ok - how would I fix that permenantly in the future - is that a skill, hook, or this project issue? I can let the engineer behind the skill/hooks know if it's a skill/hooks issue
Sep 12, 10:22 AM PDTDana Whitfield
Root cause breakdown, in order of where the actual defect sits: **1. This project: not the cause.** Nothing about the five-track structure or the CLAUDE.md fan-out is misconfigured. This would happen to anyone spawning multiple subagents with their own personas under one repo root. **2. The hook itself: working as designed, but on bad input.** The global `SessionStart` hook (`~/.claude/settings.json`) runs: ``` show_profile.py --root "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$PWD}" --autostart-only ``` It correctly displays whatever persona it finds at that root. The problem is *when* it fires: `SessionStart` runs before a subagent's first turn — before it ever gets to run `cd 01-product-industry-research-design`. At that moment both `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` and `$PWD` are still the parent project root, so the hook has no way to know the subagent is about to relocate. This isn't a bug in the hook script — it's doing exactly what it's told with the only values it has access to at fire-time. **3. The real gap: the `Agent` tool has no way to set a subagent's starting working directory.** If it did (a `cwd` parameter, distinct from `isolation: "worktree"`), a subagent could be launched already rooted in its own track directory, and this SessionStart hook would resolve correctly on its own — no manual re-run needed. As it stands, the only way to change a subagent's directory is a `cd` it runs itself, after SessionStart has already fired. **4. Secondary: profile-gen's own design.** The skill is explicitly documented as one-persona-per-project (a single picture shown via root `CLAUDE.md`'s `@import`), not built for N simultaneous personas across subdirectories of the same repo. Even with a fixed hook, it'd need a documented pattern for "resolve the persona from the nearest ancestor CLAUDE.md to `$PWD`, not always the outermost one" to support this case well. **What I'd tell the engineer:** the actionable fix is #3 — add a `cwd` parameter to the `Agent` tool so a subagent's session (and thus its `SessionStart` hooks) starts in the intended directory rather than always inheriting the parent's. That one change fixes this for any skill relying on `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`/`$PWD` at session start, not just profile-gen. I'll draft this as feedback now so you have it queued to send.

Sep 12, 10:22 AM PDTDana Whitfield
Feedback is drafted and queued locally — nothing sent yet. Review/send it with `/feedback` whenever you want, or hand the summary above directly to whoever maintains the Agent tool/hooks. **For your own future projects in the meantime**, the practical workaround is what we did here: don't rely on the `SessionStart` autostart hook for subagents at all — have each subagent's spawn prompt explicitly run `show_profile.py --profile <own-persona.md> --root <own-directory>` after it `cd`s, as its own manual step. That sidesteps the timing problem entirely regardless of whether the harness ever adds a `cwd` param.

1	# Planning Approach: Claude-Architecture Recommendations for This Team
2	
3	Author: Marcus Ilori (02-ai-security-architecture), synthesizing input gathered directly from all four other track owners — naomi-voss (01), renata-cole (03), theo-bergman (04), priya-nandakumar (05) — plus this track's own perspective and `research-claude-architecture-best-practices.md`. Where the tracks disagreed or emphasized different things, I say so rather than averaging them into a mush; where they independently converged (which happened more than I expected), I note that too, because five agents with separate contexts landing on the same answer is itself weak evidence the answer is right.
4	
5	Throughout, I distinguish Anthropic-documented guidance from my own judgment applied to this specific project. Where I say "Anthropic recommends," that traces to a specific source in `research-claude-architecture-best-practices.md`. Where I say "my view" or "I'd recommend," that's this project's reasoning, not a citation.
6	
7	## 1. Models and effort levels, by role
8	
9	The knobs: model ∈ {fable, opus, sonnet, haiku}, effort ∈ {low, medium, high, xhigh, max}. Warren has already set the orchestrator (team-lead) to fable at high. My reasoning below takes that as fixed and reasons about everyone else relative to it.
10	
11	**Orchestrator / PM (team-lead) — fable, high.** Confirmed correct, not just accepted. The orchestrator holds the only cross-track state that matters (scope boundaries, status, "who's blocked on whom") and makes judgment calls that propagate into every track if wrong — a boundary misjudged here (e.g., letting infra silently absorb a decision that was security's to make) costs five agents' worth of rework, not one. That's the single seat in this project where the highest model ceiling earns its cost. I would not go to xhigh/max for this role: xhigh/max effort spends more test-time compute on deeper deliberation per response, and orchestration work here is mostly reading condensed hand-offs and delegating — breadth of correct judgment across many small decisions, not depth on one hard problem. High is the right ceiling for that shape of work.
12	
13	**Track owners (the five of us, in planning/design mode) — sonnet, medium-to-high, track-dependent.** This is my own judgment, and it's not uniform across tracks — the five tracks are not equally deep:
14	- Product/research framing (01, Naomi) is synthesis and judgment-call writing — Naomi herself recommended sonnet/medium for her own track, and I agree: the value there is scoping and narrative tightness, not deep technical reasoning, so paying for more depth than the task uses is waste.
15	- Security (02, this track) and the engineering track's design decisions (03) involve genuine multi-step reasoning with real correctness stakes — sonnet/high is the right default there.
16	- Data-ops (05, Priya) is the one track where I'd go above sonnet during *planning*, and Priya made the argument better than I would have: the schema and DAO interface contract is precision-heavy but low-volume, and the cost of a wrong nullable-vs-required field or a wrong interface signature ripples into both 03 and 04. That's asymmetric risk, not asymmetric size — one careful opus/high pass on that specific artifact costs little in absolute tokens and avoids a class of downstream rework nothing else in this project can prevent. This is the same "spend the expensive model where a wrong foundational call is costly to reverse" principle I apply to implementation below, just moved one step upstream to where the contract is actually decided.
17	- Infra (04, Theo) explicitly self-assessed low-to-medium, sonnet, and I agree with his reasoning: it's the smallest-surface-area track, it's consumer-not-producer during planning, and over-resourcing it during the planning phase is the same mistake as over-engineering the infra itself.
18	
19	**Throwaway research subagents (fact-gathering, doc lookups, exploring existing code) — haiku, low, or sonnet/low if some judgment is needed.** Naomi's own workflow already does this (haiku/sonnet-low fanned out for industry facts, then one sonnet/medium synthesis pass) and it generalizes: per Anthropic's orchestrator-worker pattern, a subagent's exploration noise never re-enters the coordinator's context — only its condensed output does — so there's no reason to pay for a stronger model on a narrow, verifiable, throwaway task. Fan these out in parallel; that's where cheap-and-fast is unambiguously the right call.
20	
21	**Implementation (writing the actual Go code — DAO, REST handlers, connector) — sonnet, medium-to-high; opus only for one narrow piece.** Renata, who owns this work, put it as sonnet/medium-high: once upstream contracts land, this is CRUD/REST/HTTP-client code against clear specs — not novel enough for opus, but not so trivial that low-effort stubs are acceptable when "reads as intentional engineering" is the grading criterion. Tests (table-driven Go, mocked DB/HTTP) are sonnet/medium. I agree with all of that, and it changes my earlier position: I had planned to reserve opus/high for implementing the DAO's multi-database interface, but if Priya delivers the interface as actual Go signatures (see hand-off structure, §2), the hard decision has already been made upstream at opus/high and implementing against it is sonnet work. That leaves the connector's token lifecycle logic (acquire, cache, refresh, never-persist-in-plaintext) as the one implementation surface where I'd still spend opus/high — the correctness bar is a security property, not just a passing test, and the assignment's question 3 is where a subtle bug most directly contradicts this track's own threat model. Anthropic's own internal eval (research doc §3) found an Opus-lead/Sonnet-subagent configuration beat a single-agent Opus baseline by 90.2% at materially higher token cost; the lesson I take is "spend the expensive model where a wrong foundational call is costly to reverse," not "use Opus everywhere."
22	
23	**Review/verification passes — high effort always; independence scaled to stakes.** Two positions came in here, and they're compatible rather than contradictory. Renata wants a high-effort review pass on her own output before calling it done, same model — catching a busted interface boundary late costs more than the tokens. That's right for engineering surfaces. My addition, Anthropic-motivated but my own extension: their multi-agent paper's rationale for parallel independent subagents is that separation of concerns "reduces path dependency" — an agent reviewing its own output inherits its own blind spots. Resolving the two explicitly: Renata's same-session high-effort self-review is the *first* pass on everything she produces — it's cheap, it catches the interface-boundary class of bug she's worried about, and there's no reason to skip it. For the security-graded surfaces specifically (credential storage design, token handling, the auth/authz scheme, and the connector code that implements them), a *second* pass in a genuinely independent session is added on top, because those are exactly what LoginID said they're grading the reasoning quality of, and a self-review cannot by construction catch an assumption the author didn't know they were making. For everything else (infra sketches, product prose, routine handlers), the self-review alone is proportionate. This is a take-home POC, not a production deploy, and calibrating review independence to actual stakes is part of the "why," not corner-cutting.
24	
25	**Fable reserved narrowly.** I would not spread fable across all five track-owner seats — that's five top-model seats purchased for a take-home whose own cited evidence (the Opus-lead/Sonnet-worker eval) argues for concentrating the strongest model at the lead and running capable-but-cheaper models at the worker tier. The one place I'd consider a second fable seat: a final cross-track consistency pass, run once near submission, checking the five tracks' finished deliverables against each other for contradictions (the exact class of problem Naomi flagged — two tracks independently deciding what `/identity` returns). That's a single, short-lived, high-value use of the top model, not a standing role.
26	
27	## 2. Architecture pattern for planning-phase coordination
28	
29	**Default: orchestrator-worker, not peer-to-peer mesh.** This is Anthropic's own documented pattern (research doc §3 — lead agent decomposes, spawns scoped subagents, subagents report back condensed findings, lead synthesizes) and Naomi independently converged on the same recommendation from the product side: five agents cross-talking as a full mesh during planning is more collision risk than value. Status and scope decisions should route through team-lead.
30	
31	**Exception: direct track-to-track handoffs on a specific shared decision.** A pure hub-and-spoke model is wrong for one case — two tracks with a direct, narrow dependency on each other's specific decision. This track and data-ops already did this, and Priya asked that it be recorded here as the worked example: we coordinated directly on modeling `user_credential.method` as a lookup table rather than a native enum, because that's a two-party decision (her schema, my method-aware credential-storage design), not a project-wide one, and routing it through team-lead both directions would have added latency for no benefit. Renata, by contrast, explicitly doesn't want peer chatter with 01 or 04 — she wants clean hand-offs from 02 and 05 that she reads once and implements against. Both are right; the difference is whether a genuine two-party decision exists. The rule I'd formalize: point-to-point messaging is fine for a specific, bounded, two-track decision; it must still land in a durable file (see §3) as the source of truth, not live only in chat history; and it doesn't substitute for the orchestrator's overall view of status.
32	
33	**Plan mode.** Narrower than I first drafted, after Renata's input. She doesn't want plan mode for her own track — the plan already exists in the assignment text, and if upstream tracks hand her concrete contracts there's nothing left to plan that a plan-mode pass would catch. I think that's correct *conditional on the hand-offs being concrete enough* (see next paragraph); if 05 hands her a prose strategy instead of interface signatures, plan mode before the DAO becomes necessary again because she'd be making design decisions that aren't hers. So the rule: plan mode gates decisions that constrain another track — API contract shapes, schema fields, token formats, the Go interface itself — at the track that owns the decision, before it's declared stable. It does not gate implementation against an already-concrete contract. For a track whose CLAUDE.md brief is already specific and self-contained (per the root file's own stated goal), formal plan-mode approval on top of that brief is redundant — the brief already is the approved plan.
34	
35	**Fork vs. fresh scoped agent.** Fork when a task needs the current session's accumulated context and would lose something real by starting clean (e.g., resuming a security review with everything already threat-modeled in-session). Fresh scoped agent when the task is genuinely independent and carrying forward unrelated context would only dilute it (a documentation lookup, a track that hasn't started). This project's five persistent track-owner teammates are, at a coarser grain, already an instance of "fresh scoped agent": each seeded only with the root `CLAUDE.md` plus its own track file, not all five tracks' accumulated history — that's Anthropic's just-in-time retrieval principle (research doc §2, technique 4) applied at the team-structure level, not just the subagent level.
36	
37	**Hand-off structure.** Per the research doc (§2/§3), Anthropic's own subagents target roughly 1,000–2,000 tokens of condensed findings passed to a coordinator — never a raw transcript. Concretely here: hand-offs happen through each track's `CLAUDE.md` Status section and named deliverable files, referenced by relative path, exactly as the root file already mandates ("cross-reference other tracks by relative path... rather than restating their content"). A `SendMessage` between tracks signals readiness or asks a targeted question — it is not the transport for the actual content, and Renata was explicit that she wants upstream decisions written somewhere durable, not relayed verbally through team-lead.
38	
39	Priya then raised the bar on what "condensed" has to mean for a hand-off into implementation, and I'm adopting her standard project-wide: a hand-off is implementable only if the receiving track can act on it *without re-deriving the sender's reasoning*. For 05→03 that means the actual Go interface signatures (method names, parameter and return types, error semantics), the field-level schema per entity with types and nullability, and explicit notes on what is out of scope for the receiver (e.g., 03 must not invent its own PII/credential separation — that's already decided in 05's governance note). Priya's commitment is to bake that Go-shaped contract directly into `../05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md` so it's copy-pasteable, not descriptive. Prose strategy alone forces re-derivation, which is both wasted tokens and a drift risk. The same standard applies to this track's outbound hand-offs: 02→03 needs the token scheme and where it's validated, not a discussion of alternatives; 02→04 needs, per Theo's ask, an explicit secrets inventory (DB credentials, IDP credentials, signing keys) and a never-log list, because he implements delivery and does not set the requirement. Those two are follow-ups for this track once the planning pause lifts — the existing `api-auth-design.md` and `connector-security.md` contain the decisions but not yet in that receiver-shaped form.
40	
41	The replies I got to my own bounded question were all in the 300–600 word range — the condensed-finding size the research doc describes — which is mild evidence the pattern holds when the question is scoped tightly.
42	
43	**Context and cache management over a multi-hour project.** Two concrete disciplines, both sourced from the research doc's context-engineering section: (1) keep each track's `CLAUDE.md` stable within a working session — edit it at the start or end of a turn, not interleaved mid-task, because an edit to a file currently loaded in context invalidates that cached prefix (research doc §4/§5); (2) when a track's own work legitimately grows long across multiple similar passes (data-ops iterating across DB backends is the clearest case in this project), compact deliberately — write a short settled-decisions summary to a file and start the next pass fresh from that summary rather than carrying the full multi-turn history forward (§2, technique 1). Neither of these is exotic; they're the direct, mechanical application of Anthropic's documented "context rot" mitigation to this project's actual shape of work.
44	
45	**Planning dependency order across tracks.** All five tracks described their dependencies independently and the graph they describe is consistent — nobody claimed a dependency someone else denied. Assembled:
46	
47	1. **First wave, in parallel: product/research (01), security (02), data-ops (05).** None of the three blocks on another. Naomi's framing depends only on the assignment text; my threat model and auth design needed the assignment text plus the one bounded coordination point with 05 (already resolved); Priya states 05 does not block on 01 or 02. Two nuances worth naming. First, Naomi regards 01 as upstream of everyone, while Priya and Renata both say 01 can run "anytime, independently" — those reconcile: nothing *hard*-blocks on 01, but her `api-connector-design-rationale.md` should be designated the single source of truth for field/endpoint shape so engineering isn't re-deriving it, which is a soft dependency (read it before finalizing, not before starting). The assignment text already specifies the question-2/3 endpoint shapes fairly concretely, so the drift risk is real but narrower than a green-field API would carry. Second, 05 is the critical-path member of this wave — it hard-blocks 03 and partially blocks 04 — so if anything in the first wave gets priority attention, it's the schema and interface contract.
48	2. **Engineering (03) has a split start.** Renata drew a useful distinction: 05 is a hard blocker for the DAO code, but 02 is not a blocker for her early scaffolding — project structure, Go module layout, route wiring, stub handlers can begin while the auth decision is still settling, and she needs 02 only before finalizing the middleware. So 03 starts scaffolding as soon as the first wave is underway and starts the DAO the moment 05's contract is stable — "stable" meaning unlikely to be rewritten, not fully polished. Waiting for perfection would idle the track that owns the largest deliverable.
49	3. **Infra (04) is last**, exactly as Theo described his own role: reviewer/consumer during planning (read the other four tracks' files, flag anything infra-shaped so it isn't silently owned elsewhere), producer only once three specific things exist — 03's service topology, config surface, and DAO driver-selection mechanism; 02's secrets inventory and never-log list; 05's migration tool/approach. Building infra design against boundaries that don't exist yet is fiction dressed as a deliverable, in Theo's words, and I don't have a better way to put it.
50	
51	## 3. Where the shared planning artifact lives
52	
53	Three tracks offered a view and they converge. Naomi proposed a single root-level `PLANNING.md` with one section per track, each track editing only its own. Theo wants one owner per section, no shared free-text blob five agents edit concurrently, and suggested a single index file linking to each track's own planning notes — matching the existing directory structure. Renata wants upstream decisions in the owning track's `CLAUDE.md` or a shared design doc she reads once. The common thread is one-owner-per-unit and a thin index, and I'd adopt Theo's shape over Naomi's for two reasons: first, even with an "own section" discipline, five agents live-editing one shared file in a short window is still a collision surface that a table of independent rows avoids more cleanly than prose sections do; second, and more importantly, this project already has a durable decision-record layer — each track's own `CLAUDE.md` plus its deliverable files — and duplicating design content into a second file risks the two drifting apart, which is a worse failure mode than the collision risk it's meant to solve.
54	
55	**Recommendation:** a root-level `PLANNING.md`, purely as a thin cross-track coordination board — one table row per track: status, blocking-on, blocked-by, pointer to that track's planning notes, last-updated — plus, per Theo's specific ask, a single "service boundaries" row or short section that 03 owns and 04 reads, so infra isn't polling engineering's `CLAUDE.md` for updates. Short, cheap to write, cheap to skim, genuinely low collision risk because each row has exactly one writer. The actual reasoning — the "why," which is most of what LoginID is grading — stays where it already lives, in each track's own files, referenced by relative path. This keeps the artifact consistent with the root `CLAUDE.md`'s existing rule (cross-reference, don't restate) instead of introducing a second place the same content has to be kept in sync.
56

Suggested anchors: red/blue on the connector token lifecycle (02/03); three hats on the multi-DB abstraction (05); adversarial pair on search-API authz scoping (02→03); newcomer's-question pass on the product framing (01); Theo's track is small — at most one hire and one pattern.\n- *Dependencies* — what this track needs from others and provides to others, consistent with the planning-approach.md order (01/02/05 first wave with 05 on the critical path; 03 split start; 04 last on three named inputs).\n\nTrack-specific additions:\n- **02 (Marcus)** additionally writes `02-ai-security-architecture/orchestration-patterns.md` — the full protocol for each pattern in the catalog (roles, sequence, artifact produced, when to use, cost in agent-turns, and where it would be waste in this project). Therefore hires must **never** be added as CLAUDE.md markers — only the five leads and Dana keep markers. Hires are displayed on demand with `show_profile.py --profile <path> --root <track-dir>`.\n- Schema has no history/backstory field (`assets/profile.schema.json`, `additionalProperties: false`); `personality` is unlimited free prose, so history + technical background live there.\n- `write_profile.py` only checks the `image` **key** exists, never the file (`write_profile.py:63,90-92`) → personas can be created **text-first** with a pre-declared image path, and the PNG generated later into that path by a background queue. Display degrades gracefully to name-only until the file exists.\n- `generate_image.py --workflow <any path> --seed N` — the existing validated workflow `profiles/dana-whitfield/comfyui-workflow.json` is reusable for every portrait; it is never mutated.\n- Global `SessionStart` hook (`~/.claude/settings.json`) runs `show_profile.py --root \"${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$PWD}\" --autostart-only` before a subagent's first turn, so every new pane shows Dana until the agent runs `show_profile.py --profile <own>`; every spawn prompt must include that command after its `cd`.\n\nHarness (confirmed against the Claude Code docs — sub-agents.md, agent-teams.md, settings-reference.md, hooks.md):\n- Agent definitions `.claude/agents/<slug>.md` support frontmatter `name`, `description`, `model` (`sonnet|opus|haiku|fable|inherit`), `tools`/`disallowedTools`, `permissionMode`, `skills`, `memory`, `maxTurns`, and a `hooks` block scoped to that agent's lifetime. **No effort key exists**, and teammates inherit the lead's effort level (Warren's `high`). The only per-model lever is `modelSettings.<model>.effortLevel` in `.claude/settings.json`. Consequence: Marcus's effort tiers are implemented as *inherit high for sonnet/opus/fable* plus `haiku → low` for throwaway research fan-out; the sonnet-medium vs sonnet-high distinction collapses to high (small cost, errs upward on the tracks that matter). Model per hire is still pinned in its definition.\n- The per-agent `hooks` block is the permanent fix for the \"every pane shows Dana\" problem: each hire's definition carries a `SessionStart` hook running `show_profile.py --clear` then `show_profile.py --profile <own persona> --root <track-dir>`, so the hire displays itself without relying on its first turn. The spawn prompt keeps the same command as a fallback.\n- Nested spawning works but is capped at 3 levels by default (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH`). Hierarchy therefore stays **team-lead → lead → hire**; hires do not spawn subagents — any research fan-out is done by the lead. The harness opens a tmux pane per spawned agent.\n- Teammate plan mode **auto-approves**: a teammate's `ExitPlanMode` is approved in the lead's session without review (agent-teams.md). So plan mode is used for what it is good at — forcing a written plan before any edits — and the real gate is manual: the lead copies its approved plan into `<track-dir>/PLAN.md`, messages team-lead, and does **not** hire until team-lead replies with an explicit \"hiring go\" after reviewing the roster against the variance rules.\n\n## Plan\n\n### Phase 0 — Team-lead lays the planning scaffolding (before any lead plans)\n\nAll files are prose; none is code. Root-level files are owned by team-lead only.\n\n1. **`PLANNING.md`** (root, new) — exactly the thin board Marcus specified: one row per track — `Track | Lead | Status | Blocking on | Blocked by | Planning notes (relative path) | Hires (count/registered) | Last updated` — plus a **Service boundaries** section that 03 owns and 04 reads (initially \"TBD — set by 03 during planning\"). One writer per row (the lead); team-lead writes the header and the two Dana rows (orchestrator, cross-track consistency pass). No reasoning in this file — pointers only.\n\n2. **`CASTING.md`** (root, new) — the org-wide casting registry, hiring rules, and orchestration-pattern catalog. Team-lead owns the rules and palette; each lead owns its own registry rows. Sections:\n\n   - *Archetype palette* (13 — enough slack for a no-repeat-within-team rule at ~11 hires):\n\n     | Archetype | Temperament sketch | Blind spot | Pairs well with |\n     |---|---|---|---|\n     | Principled Architect | standards-first, cites RFC section numbers, reasons threat-model-down | over-specifies; mistakes thoroughness for done | security controls, contracts |\n     | Tinkerer (silly & innovative) | playful, analogy-driven, sketches three odd alternatives before defending one | novelty bias; underweights boring proven controls | any decision that needs alternatives |\n     | Designated Skeptic | argues against the room by default, politely | objections without a counter-proposal | authz scoping, abstraction choices |\n     | Consensus Weaver | finds the shared premise, writes the synthesis | papers over real disagreement | decision records, hand-off prose |\n     | Spreadsheet | wants a number or a table; distrusts adjectives | measures only what is measurable | sizing, TTLs, retention windows |\n     | Storyteller | thinks in user journeys and narrative arcs | charming beats correct | product framing, README, use cases |\n     | Veteran | \"I watched this fail in prod in 2019\" | fights the last war; pattern-matches too fast | token lifecycle, migrations, ops |\n     | Newcomer | bright, asks why, assumes no jargon | doesn't know what's already settled | legibility tests on every hand-off |\n     | Detail Hawk | nullable-vs-required, off-by-one, error semantics | cannot see the forest | schema, DDL, code review |\n     | Systems Cartographer | draws boundaries and flows; sees interactions | abstracts past the concrete | multi-DB abstraction, service topology |\n     | Adversary | thinks like an attacker, enjoys it | sees threats everywhere, no prioritization | connector, search API |\n     | Minimalist | \"what if we didn't build this?\" | deletes something load-bearing | infra, scope control |\n     | Enthusiast | bold, optimistic, wants the promising thing tried | discounts operational cost | counterweight to deadpan/cautious leads |\n\n   - *Variance rules* (team-lead enforces at the hiring gate, reading all rosters together): (1) no archetype more than **2× org-wide**, never twice within a team; (2) no two leads hire the same archetype for the same function (two \"Detail Hawk reviewers\" is duplication, not variance); (3) every team includes its lead's **temperament opposite** — Naomi (curious narrative analyst) → Spreadsheet; Marcus (rigorous citer) → Tinkerer; Renata (clarity-over-cleverness shipper) → Enthusiast; Theo (deadpan minimalist) → Enthusiast or Newcomer; Priya (schema-first PII guardian) → Storyteller; (4) org-wide at least **three** hires tagged playful+bold, because zero leads are; (5) each roster spans at least two temperament axes (serious↔playful, cautious↔bold).\n\n   - *Sizing* (Warren's call: the larger org) — 3–4 hires per track, 18 total, **hard cap 18**. With 13 archetypes at ≤2× org-wide there are 26 slots, so the cap fits without repeats inside any team. Suggested cast, leads may adjust within the rules: 01 (3) → Storyteller, Spreadsheet (opposite), Designated Skeptic; 02 (4) → Adversary, Principled Architect, Tinkerer (opposite), Designated Skeptic; 03 (4) → Detail Hawk (code reviewer), Veteran, Enthusiast (opposite), Systems Cartographer (Go layout / service topology); 04 (3) → Newcomer, Enthusiast (opposite), Minimalist (scope guard); 05 (4) → Systems Cartographer (multi-DB abstraction), Storyteller (opposite), Detail Hawk (DDL nullability — a different function from 03's, so allowed), Spreadsheet (retention windows). Archetypes used twice must have different functions. Leads may still wear a \"hat\" themselves in three-hats runs rather than hiring for it.\n\n   - *Persona-writing standard* — 90–150 words in `personality`, in this order: temperament (two adjectives and how they show up in a meeting); decision style; **what they specifically push back on**; plausible history (two prior roles, generic employers, no real names); technical background tied to the function; **their own blind spot stated plainly** so the lead can pair against it; how they behave in disagreement. One verbal habit allowed (a recurring question, an analogy style); no repeated catchphrases. Avoid: real people/companies, checkable credentials, stereotypes, sarcasm at a person's expense, profanity, emoji, appearance (that belongs in the image prompt). Two worked examples are included verbatim in `CASTING.md` — the Tinkerer (\"a token cache is a coat check that burns the coats every fifteen minutes… pushes back on 'that's the standard approach' whenever nobody can say what the standard protects against; blind spot: novelty bias\") and the Principled Architect (\"will not accept a control without naming the attack it stops… blind spot: over-specification; changes position when shown evidence, and says so\") — so the contrast Warren asked for is concrete.\n\n   - *Orchestration pattern catalog* (full protocols; cost in agent-turns = one hire invocation + reply):\n     - **Adversarial pair** — Proposer drafts; Designated Skeptic writes numbered objections; Proposer revises; lead rules. 3 turns + lead. Artifact: decision with \"objections considered\". *Use:* search-API authz scoping (`profile:search` vs `profile:read:own`, object-level policy); the `Search()` query shape in `multi-db-strategy.md`.\n     - **Red team / blue team** — Adversary writes an attack tree against one surface; Principled Architect answers each leaf with a control or an accepted risk; lead scores residual risk. 2–3 turns. Artifact: attack-tree table appended to `connector-security.md`. *Use:* the connector token lifecycle only (TTL, at-rest encryption, per-vendor keying, fail-closed). Waste on DAO CRUD or a Dockerfile.\n     - **Three hats** — Tinkerer (optimist), Veteran (pessimist), Spreadsheet/Minimalist (pragmatist) each write ≤300 words in parallel; lead synthesizes. 3 turns + lead. *Use:* multi-DB abstraction (per-driver implementations vs dialect branches vs codegen); Go layout (one binary vs two).\n     - **Rotating devil's advocate** — a standing \"Objection\" seat that moves per decision so nobody becomes \"the negative one\". 1 turn per decision. *Use:* Renata's implementation calls (error semantics, context propagation, test doubles). Cheapest pattern; run often.\n     - **Structured written debate** — position → rebuttal → lead synthesis, both positions preserved. 3 turns. *Use:* Naomi's central framing (password baseline: deliberate simplification or contradiction at a passkey company?); Marcus's core claim in `ai-workflow-narrative.md`.\n     - **Newcomer's question** — Newcomer reads a finished hand-off cold and lists what it could not act on without re-deriving; author fixes. 1–2 turns. *Use:* every 05→03 and 02→03 hand-off, the README, `PLANNING.md`. Directly tests the hand-off standard; highest-ROI pattern in the project.\n     - *Never debate what the assignment text fixes* (endpoint paths, JSON bodies). Theo's track: one hire, one Newcomer's-question pass on 03's config surface and 02's secrets inventory, nothing else.\n\n   - *Registry table* — one row per hire, appended by the hiring lead after team-lead's \"hiring go\": `Slug | Name | Track | Function | Archetype | Temperament tags | Model | Patterns & role | Persona path | Agent def path | Portrait (queued/done)`.\n\n3. **`.claude/agents/`** (root, new directory) — one `<slug>.md` per hire. Frontmatter: `name`, `description` (function + track + archetype, so the Agent tool picker reads it), `model` (pinned per Marcus's tiers: haiku for research fan-out, sonnet for design/debate/implementation, opus only for the connector token-lifecycle seat), `tools` narrowed where sensible (debate/research hires: no Write/Edit — they return prose to the lead, who writes the artifact), and a `hooks` block with a `SessionStart` command that runs `show_profile.py --clear` then `show_profile.py --profile <own persona> --root <track-dir>`. Body (plain markdown, no `@` imports — not documented for agent bodies): the persona paragraph copied from its profile, pointers to root and track `CLAUDE.md`, the pattern(s) and role it plays, and the hard rules (`cd <track-dir>` first; no code until Warren's go-ahead; hand-offs by file path; do not spawn subagents — depth cap). Team-lead writes `.claude/agents/_TEMPLATE.md`; leads copy it per hire.\n\n3a. **`.claude/settings.json`** (project, new) — `modelSettings.haiku.effortLevel: \"low\"` so throwaway research fan-out runs cheap; everything else inherits Warren's `high`. Done via the `update-config` skill. ## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Org-wide planning phase for the LoginID take-home ("hire" the sub-teams, plan every track, then Jira)

## Context

The LoginID take-home is being run as a demonstration of AI-architecture capability: a root PM agent (Dana Whitfield / team-lead) plus five persistent track leads (Naomi Voss 01, Marcus Ilori 02, Renata Cole 03, Theo Bergman 04, Priya Nandakumar 05), each with a profile-gen persona, each working from its own track directory. All five tracks have finished their first-wave design docs; all code has been deliberately wiped; the project is paused in a design/planning phase. Marcus has delivered the team's agreed Claude-architecture approach in `02-ai-security-architecture/planning-approach.md` (model/effort per role, orchestrator-worker with bounded two-party exceptions, the "receiver can act without re-deriving" hand-off standard, dependency order 01/02/05 → 03 → 04, and a thin root `PLANNING.md` board).

Warren now wants the formal planning phase run across the whole org, with three additions to Marcus's approach:

1. Every track lead must "hire" (create) a sub-team of agents and state each hire's function. Hires must vary in **personality and archetype**, not just viewpoint — variance across the org, not just within a team (a serious principles-based architect paired with a silly innovative one, etc.).
2. **Every** agent created must run profile-gen so it has a personality, professional history, and technical background. Profile pictures are optional and may be produced by background jobs.
3. The orchestration patterns must make heavy use of those personas to drive debate and diversity of thought.

Leads may spawn their sub-teams into new tmux panes. Everyone goes into plan mode and plans first; Jira stories in project `LT` (uplifttech.atlassian.net, cloudId `db488eae-d6cb-4100-92a9-e7a4519fc176`, issue types Epic/Story/Task/Bug/Subtask) come **after** planning. No implementation code until Warren gives a separate go-ahead.

Everything in `/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID` is part of the submission, so the org scaffolding (casting registry, personas, agent definitions) must read as intentional, presentable AI-architecture work — not scratch.

## Inputs this plan builds on (already decided — do not re-litigate)

- `02-ai-security-architecture/planning-approach.md` — models/effort by role (orchestrator fable/high; leads sonnet, medium for 01/04, high for 02/03, **opus/high for 05 planning**; research subagents haiku or sonnet/low; implementation sonnet/medium-high, opus/high only for the connector token lifecycle; review = author self-review first, independent second pass on security-graded surfaces only; fable for a single final cross-track consistency pass), coordination rules, hand-off standard, dependency order, and the thin `PLANNING.md` board shape.
- Root `CLAUDE.md` — track ownership table and the "cross-reference, don't restate" rule.
- profile-gen mechanics verified in this session (see "Hire creation mechanics" below).

## Verified mechanics that shape the design

profile-gen (`~/.claude/skills/profile-gen` → symlink to `~/Source/ProfileGenSkill`):
- `--output file --assets tracked --root <track-dir>` writes `<track-dir>/profiles/<slug>/<slug>.md` with **no** CLAUDE.md line; unlimited personas per directory (`scripts/profilegen/storage.py:98-110`, `write_profile.py:121-142`).
- `show_profile.py --autostart-only` displays **every** persona that has a `profile-gen:start` marker in the root `CLAUDE.md`, in file order (`show_profile.py:219-224`). Therefore hires must **never** be added as CLAUDE.md markers — only the five leads and Dana keep markers. Hires are displayed on demand with `show_profile.py --profile <path> --root <track-dir>`.
- Schema has no history/backstory field (`assets/profile.schema.json`, `additionalProperties: false`); `personality` is unlimited free prose, so history + technical background live there.
- `write_profile.py` only checks the `image` **key** exists, never the file (`write_profile.py:63,90-92`) → personas can be created **text-first** with a pre-declared image path, and the PNG generated later into that path by a background queue. Display degrades gracefully to name-only until the file exists.
- `generate_image.py --workflow <any path> --seed N` — the existing validated workflow `profiles/dana-whitfield/comfyui-workflow.json` is reusable for every portrait; it is never mutated.
- Global `SessionStart` hook (`~/.claude/settings.json`) runs `show_profile.py --root "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$PWD}" --autostart-only` before a subagent's first turn, so every new pane shows Dana until the agent runs `show_profile.py --profile <own>`; every spawn prompt must include that command after its `cd`.

Harness (confirmed against the Claude Code docs — sub-agents.md, agent-teams.md, settings-reference.md, hooks.md):
- Agent definitions `.claude/agents/<slug>.md` support frontmatter `name`, `description`, `model` (`sonnet|opus|haiku|fable|inherit`), `tools`/`disallowedTools`, `permissionMode`, `skills`, `memory`, `maxTurns`, and a `hooks` block scoped to that agent's lifetime. **No effort key exists**, and teammates inherit the lead's effort level (Warren's `high`). The only per-model lever is `modelSettings.<model>.effortLevel` in `.claude/settings.json`. Consequence: Marcus's effort tiers are implemented as *inherit high for sonnet/opus/fable* plus `haiku → low` for throwaway research fan-out; the sonnet-medium vs sonnet-high distinction collapses to high (small cost, errs upward on the tracks that matter). Model per hire is still pinned in its definition.
- The per-agent `hooks` block is the permanent fix for the "every pane shows Dana" problem: each hire's definition carries a `SessionStart` hook running `show_profile.py --clear` then `show_profile.py --profile <own persona> --root <track-dir>`, so the hire displays itself without relying on its first turn. The spawn prompt keeps the same command as a fallback.
- Nested spawning works but is capped at 3 levels by default (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH`). Hierarchy therefore stays **team-lead → lead → hire**; hires do not spawn subagents — any research fan-out is done by the lead. The harness opens a tmux pane per spawned agent.
- Teammate plan mode **auto-approves**: a teammate's `ExitPlanMode` is approved in the lead's session without review (agent-teams.md). So plan mode is used for what it is good at — forcing a written plan before any edits — and the real gate is manual: the lead copies its approved plan into `<track-dir>/PLAN.md`, messages team-lead, and does **not** hire until team-lead replies with an explicit "hiring go" after reviewing the roster against the variance rules.

## Plan

### Phase 0 — Team-lead lays the planning scaffolding (before any lead plans)

All files are prose; none is code. Root-level files are owned by team-lead only.

**0. Make the process itself part of the submission.** Warren wants every step and all progress saved so it can be turned in; today the org plan lives only at `~/.claude/plans/cuddly-nibbling-cherny.md`, outside the repo, and the leads' plan-mode files would land there too. Three fixes, all in-repo:
   - **`PLAN.md`** (root, new) — this plan, copied verbatim into the repo and kept as the living org plan (updated at phase boundaries, not mid-task). Leads' plans go to `<track-dir>/PLAN.md` (already required in Phase 1). `~/.claude/plans/` is treated as scratch from here on.
   - **`LOG.md`** (root, new) — a dated, append-only progress journal owned by team-lead: one entry per milestone or decision (what happened, who, which files changed, why), backfilled from this session's history so far — scaffolding of the five tracks, the six research passes and their key findings (incl. the Dick Hardt/aauth.dev correction and LoginID's agentic-commerce repositioning), persona generation, the code wipe and return to planning, Marcus's planning-approach synthesis, and this plan. Leads append one line to their own `PLANNING.md` row instead; cross-track events go in `LOG.md` via team-lead. Each debate run's artifact (Phase 3) is itself part of the record.
   - **`git init`** at the root with a milestone commit at the end of every phase (Phase 0 scaffolding, each approved plan, each completed hiring wave, each debate artifact set, the consistency pass, Jira export). The commit history is the most credible "how the AI workflow actually ran" artifact the submission can carry; commit messages follow the repo attribution convention (Co-Authored-By Claude Fable 5.1 + session link), which itself documents the AI involvement LoginID asked about. Commits only at milestones, never mid-task, and never `.claude/settings.local.json`, image caches, or secrets. A `.gitignore` covers `.claude/settings.local.json` and `*.lock`.

1. **`PLANNING.md`** (root, new) — exactly the thin board Marcus specified: one row per track — `Track | Lead | Status | Blocking on | Blocked by | Planning notes (relative path) | Hires (count/registered) | Last updated` — plus a **Service boundaries** section that 03 owns and 04 reads (initially "TBD — set by 03 during planning"). One writer per row (the lead); team-lead writes the header and the two Dana rows (orchestrator, cross-track consistency pass). No reasoning in this file — pointers only.

2. **`CASTING.md`** (root, new) — the org-wide casting registry, hiring rules, and orchestration-pattern catalog. Team-lead owns the rules and palette; each lead owns its own registry rows. Sections:

   - *Archetype palette* (13 — enough slack for a no-repeat-within-team rule at ~11 hires):

     | Archetype | Temperament sketch | Blind spot | Pairs well with |
     |---|---|---|---|
     | Principled Architect | standards-first, cites RFC section numbers, reasons threat-model-down | over-specifies; mistakes thoroughness for done | security controls, contracts |
     | Tinkerer (silly & innovative) | playful, analogy-driven, sketches three odd alternatives before defending one | novelty bias; underweights boring proven controls | any decision that needs alternatives |
     | Designated Skeptic | argues against the room by default, politely | objections without a counter-proposal | authz scoping, abstraction choices |
     | Consensus Weaver | finds the shared premise, writes the synthesis | papers over real disagreement | decision records, hand-off prose |
     | Spreadsheet | wants a number or a table; distrusts adjectives | measures only what is measurable | sizing, TTLs, retention windows |
     | Storyteller | thinks in user journeys and narrative arcs | charming beats correct | product framing, README, use cases |
     | Veteran | "I watched this fail in prod in 2019" | fights the last war; pattern-matches too fast | token lifecycle, migrations, ops |
     | Newcomer | bright, asks why, assumes no jargon | doesn't know what's already settled | legibility tests on every hand-off |
     | Detail Hawk | nullable-vs-required, off-by-one, error semantics | cannot see the forest | schema, DDL, code review |
     | Systems Cartographer | draws boundaries and flows; sees interactions | abstracts past the concrete | multi-DB abstraction, service topology |
     | Adversary | thinks like an attacker, enjoys it | sees threats everywhere, no prioritization | connector, search API |
     | Minimalist | "what if we didn't build this?" | deletes something load-bearing | infra, scope control |
     | Enthusiast | bold, optimistic, wants the promising thing tried | discounts operational cost | counterweight to deadpan/cautious leads |

   - *Variance rules* (team-lead enforces at the hiring gate, reading all rosters together): (1) no archetype more than **2× org-wide**, never twice within a team; (2) no two leads hire the same archetype for the same function (two "Detail Hawk reviewers" is duplication, not variance); (3) every team includes its lead's **temperament opposite** — Naomi (curious narrative analyst) → Spreadsheet; Marcus (rigorous citer) → Tinkerer; Renata (clarity-over-cleverness shipper) → Enthusiast; Theo (deadpan minimalist) → Enthusiast or Newcomer; Priya (schema-first PII guardian) → Storyteller; (4) org-wide at least **three** hires tagged playful+bold, because zero leads are; (5) each roster spans at least two temperament axes (serious↔playful, cautious↔bold).

   - *Sizing* (Warren's call: the larger org) — 3–4 hires per track, 18 total, **hard cap 18**. With 13 archetypes at ≤2× org-wide there are 26 slots, so the cap fits without repeats inside any team. Suggested cast, leads may adjust within the rules: 01 (3) → Storyteller, Spreadsheet (opposite), Designated Skeptic; 02 (4) → Adversary, Principled Architect, Tinkerer (opposite), Designated Skeptic; 03 (4) → Detail Hawk (code reviewer), Veteran, Enthusiast (opposite), Systems Cartographer (Go layout / service topology); 04 (3) → Newcomer, Enthusiast (opposite), Minimalist (scope guard); 05 (4) → Systems Cartographer (multi-DB abstraction), Storyteller (opposite), Detail Hawk (DDL nullability — a different function from 03's, so allowed), Spreadsheet (retention windows). Archetypes used twice must have different functions. Leads may still wear a "hat" themselves in three-hats runs rather than hiring for it.

   - *Persona-writing standard* — 90–150 words in `personality`, in this order: temperament (two adjectives and how they show up in a meeting); decision style; **what they specifically push back on**; plausible history (two prior roles, generic employers, no real names); technical background tied to the function; **their own blind spot stated plainly** so the lead can pair against it; how they behave in disagreement. One verbal habit allowed (a recurring question, an analogy style); no repeated catchphrases. Avoid: real people/companies, checkable credentials, stereotypes, sarcasm at a person's expense, profanity, emoji, appearance (that belongs in the image prompt). Two worked examples are included verbatim in `CASTING.md` — the Tinkerer ("a token cache is a coat check that burns the coats every fifteen minutes… pushes back on 'that's the standard approach' whenever nobody can say what the standard protects against; blind spot: novelty bias") and the Principled Architect ("will not accept a control without naming the attack it stops… blind spot: over-specification; changes position when shown evidence, and says so") — so the contrast Warren asked for is concrete.

   - *Orchestration pattern catalog* (full protocols; cost in agent-turns = one hire invocation + reply):
     - **Adversarial pair** — Proposer drafts; Designated Skeptic writes numbered objections; Proposer revises; lead rules. 3 turns + lead. Artifact: decision with "objections considered". *Use:* search-API authz scoping (`profile:search` vs `profile:read:own`, object-level policy); the `Search()` query shape in `multi-db-strategy.md`.
     - **Red team / blue team** — Adversary writes an attack tree against one surface; Principled Architect answers each leaf with a control or an accepted risk; lead scores residual risk. 2–3 turns. Artifact: attack-tree table appended to `connector-security.md`. *Use:* the connector token lifecycle only (TTL, at-rest encryption, per-vendor keying, fail-closed). Waste on DAO CRUD or a Dockerfile.
     - **Three hats** — Tinkerer (optimist), Veteran (pessimist), Spreadsheet/Minimalist (pragmatist) each write ≤300 words in parallel; lead synthesizes. 3 turns + lead. *Use:* multi-DB abstraction (per-driver implementations vs dialect branches vs codegen); Go layout (one binary vs two).
     - **Rotating devil's advocate** — a standing "Objection" seat that moves per decision so nobody becomes "the negative one". 1 turn per decision. *Use:* Renata's implementation calls (error semantics, context propagation, test doubles). Cheapest pattern; run often.
     - **Structured written debate** — position → rebuttal → lead synthesis, both positions preserved. 3 turns. *Use:* Naomi's central framing (password baseline: deliberate simplification or contradiction at a passkey company?); Marcus's core claim in `ai-workflow-narrative.md`.
     - **Newcomer's question** — Newcomer reads a finished hand-off cold and lists what it could not act on without re-deriving; author fixes. 1–2 turns. *Use:* every 05→03 and 02→03 hand-off, the README, `PLANNING.md`. Directly tests the hand-off standard; highest-ROI pattern in the project.

  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/CASTING.md",
  "content": "# Casting Registry — hiring rules, archetypes, and orchestration patterns\n\nEvery agent in this project is a \"hire\" with a profile-gen persona: a name, a temperament, a plausible professional history, a technical background, and a stated blind spot. This file is how the org keeps those hires **varied across teams, not just within one** — five leads who all hire clones of themselves would produce agreement, and agreement is not what a design review is for. Team-lead (Dana Whitfield) owns everything above the registry table; each lead owns its own rows.\n\nWhy this exists, in one sentence: the five track leads and the PM all sit in the same temperament quadrant (serious, careful, rigorous), so variance has to be engineered in deliberately or it will not happen.\n\n## 1. Archetype palette\n\nThirteen archetypes. Each has a characteristic blind spot on purpose — the lead pairs against it. Playful and quirky is a legitimate professional register here; caricature is not.\n\n| Archetype | Temperament sketch | Blind spot | Pairs well with |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Principled Architect | standards-first, cites RFC section numbers, reasons threat-model-down | over-specifies; mistakes thoroughness for done | security controls, contracts |\n| Tinkerer (silly & innovative) | playful, analogy-driven, sketches three odd alternatives before defending one | novelty bias; underweights boring proven controls | any decision that needs alternatives |\n| Designated Skeptic | argues against the room by default, politely | objections without a counter-proposal | authz scoping, abstraction choices |\n| Consensus Weaver | finds the shared premise, writes the synthesis | papers over real disagreement | decision records, hand-off prose |\n| Spreadsheet | wants a number or a table; distrusts adjectives | measures only what is measurable | sizing, TTLs, retention windows |\n| Storyteller | thinks in user journeys and narrative arcs | charming beats correct | product framing, README, use cases |\n| Veteran | \"I watched this fail in prod in 2019\" | fights the last war; pattern-matches too fast | token lifecycle, migrations, ops |\n| Newcomer | bright, asks why, assumes no jargon | doesn't know what's already settled | legibility tests on every hand-off |\n| Detail Hawk | nullable-vs-required, off-by-one, error semantics | cannot see the forest | schema, DDL, code review |\n| Systems Cartographer | draws boundaries and flows; sees interactions | abstracts past the concrete | multi-DB abstraction, service topology |\n| Adversary | thinks like an attacker, enjoys it | sees threats everywhere, no prioritization | connector, search API |\n| Minimalist | \"what if we didn't build this?\" | deletes something load-bearing | infra, scope control |\n| Enthusiast | bold, optimistic, wants the promising thing tried | discounts operational cost | counterweight to deadpan/cautious leads |\n\nTemperament tags used in the registry: `serious` / `playful`, `cautious` / `bold`.\n\n## 2. Variance rules (enforced by team-lead at the hiring gate, reading all rosters together)\n\n1. No archetype more than **twice org-wide**, and never twice within one team.\n2. No two leads hire the same archetype for the same **function** — two \"Detail Hawk reviewers\" is duplication, not variance. An archetype used twice must do different work (e.g., Detail Hawk as code reviewer in 03 and as DDL/nullability reviewer in 05).\n3. Every team includes its lead's **temperament opposite**: Naomi (curious narrative analyst) → Spreadsheet; Marcus (rigorous citer) → Tinkerer; Renata (clarity-over-cleverness shipper) → Enthusiast; Theo (deadpan minimalist) → Enthusiast or Newcomer; Priya (schema-first PII guardian) → Storyteller.\n4. Org-wide, at least **three** hires are tagged `playful` + `bold`, because zero leads are.\n5. Each roster spans at least two temperament axes (serious↔playful and cautious↔bold), not one.\n6. Rosters are approved in arrival order; later leads take what the palette has left. Team-lead breaks ties.\n\n## 3. Sizing\n\n3–4 hires per track, **18 total, hard cap 18** (Warren's call). Thirteen archetypes at ≤2× each is 26 slots, so the cap fits with no repeats inside any team. Suggested cast — leads may adjust within the rules:\n\n| Track | Hires | Suggested cast |\n|---|---|---|\n| 01 Product & Industry Research | 3 | Storyteller · Spreadsheet (lead's opposite) · Designated Skeptic |\n| 02 AI & Security Architecture | 4 | Adversary · Principled Architect · Tinkerer (lead's opposite) · Designated Skeptic |\n| 03 Engineering & Delivery | 4 | Detail Hawk (code reviewer) · Veteran · Enthusiast (lead's opposite) · Systems Cartographer (Go layout / service topology) |\n| 04 Infra & DevOps | 3 | Newcomer · Enthusiast (lead's opposite) · Minimalist (scope guard) |\n| 05 Data Ops | 4 | Systems Cartographer (multi-DB abstraction) · Storyteller (lead's opposite) · Detail Hawk (DDL nullability) · Spreadsheet (retention windows) |\n\nLeads may wear a \"hat\" themselves in a three-hats run rather than hiring for it. Hires do **not** spawn subagents (nested-spawn depth cap); any research fan-out is done by the lead with haiku/low throwaway subagents.\n\n## 4. Persona-writing standard\n\nThe `personality` field of a profile-gen persona is free prose and is the only place for history and technical background (the schema has no separate field), so write it to carry all of it. **90–150 words**, in this order:\n\n1. Temperament — two adjectives and how they show up in a meeting.\n2. Decision style.\n3. **What they specifically push back on.**\n4. Plausible history — two prior roles, generic employers, no real names.\n5. Technical background tied to the function they are hired for.\n6. **Their own blind spot, stated plainly**, so the lead can pair against it.\n7. How they behave in disagreement.\n\nOne verbal habit is allowed (a recurring question, an analogy style); catchphrases repeated every message are not. Avoid: real people or companies, checkable credentials, stereotypes, sarcasm at a person's expense, profanity, emoji, and anything about appearance (that belongs in the portrait prompt, not the persona). No two hires in the org may share a first name with a lead or with each other.\n\n**Worked example — Tinkerer (playful, bold):**\n\n> Playful, fast-associating security architect who thinks out loud in analogies — a token cache is \"a coat check that burns the coats every fifteen minutes\" — and reaches for the odd angle first: what if the connector held no token at all? Six years building fraud-detection pipelines at a payments startup, three at a mid-size IAM vendor, mostly Go and Rust; his prototypes broke in ways nobody had thought to test for, which was the point. Decides by sketching three alternatives before defending one, and cheerfully drops his own when shown a cleaner failure mode. Pushes back on \"that's the standard approach\" whenever nobody can say what the standard protects against. Blind spot: novelty bias — he underweights boring, proven controls, so pair him with someone who cites the RFC. Warm in disagreement, never sarcastic.\n\n**Worked example — Principled Architect (serious, cautious):**\n\n> Measured, standards-first security architect who reasons from the threat model down and will not accept a control without naming the attack it stops. Twelve years across a bank's identity platform and a public-sector PKI program; deep in OAuth2/OIDC, JOSE, and the failure modes of JWT libraries, reads RFCs as primary sources and quotes section numbers. Decides slowly and writes decisions as records with alternatives considered. Pushes back on any trade of a security property for convenience, and on claims without a source. Blind spot: over-specification — she designs for threats the assignment doesn't have and can mistake thoroughness for finishedness, so pair her with a pragmatist asking what the take-home actually needs. Courteous, unhurried, unbothered by challenge; changes position when shown evidence, and says so.\n\n## 5. Orchestration pattern catalog\n\nEach pattern is a small protocol: roles, sequence, the artifact it produces, when it earns its cost, and where it is waste. Cost is in agent-turns (one hire invocation and reply). Every run ends in a durable file in the track directory (`<track>/decisions/<topic>.md`: positions, rebuttals, the lead's synthesis and decision) — chat is not the record. The AI-architecture owner (02) ratifies and may amend this catalog.\n\n**Adversarial pair.** Proposer drafts; Designated Skeptic writes numbered objections; Proposer revises; lead rules. 3 turns + lead. Artifact: the decision with an \"objections considered\" section. *Use:* search-API authorization scoping (`profile:search` vs `profile:read:own`, object-level policy); the `Search()` query shape in `05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md`.\n\n**Red team / blue team.** Adversary writes an attack tree against one surface; Principled Architect answers each leaf with a control or an explicitly accepted risk; lead scores residual risk. 2–3 turns. Artifact: attack-tree table appended to `02-ai-security-architecture/connector-security.md`. *Use:* the connector token lifecycle only (TTL, at-rest encryption, per-vendor keying, fail-closed behavior). *Waste:* DAO CRUD, Dockerfiles.\n\n**Three hats.** Tinkerer (optimist), Veteran (pessimist), Spreadsheet or Minimalist (pragmatist) each write ≤300 words on one choice, in parallel; lead synthesizes. 3 turns + lead. *Use:* the multi-DB abstraction (per-driver implementations vs. dialect branches vs. codegen); Go layout (one binary vs. two). A lead may take a hat.\n\n**Rotating devil's advocate.** A standing \"Objection\" seat that moves to a different hire per decision so nobody becomes \"the negative one\". 1 turn per decision, appended as a short Objection block. *Use:* 03's implementation calls (error semantics, context propagation, test doubles). Cheapest pattern; run it often.\n\n**Structured written debate.** Position → rebuttal → lead synthesis, with both positions preserved in the record. 3 turns. *Use:* 01's central framing (is a password baseline a deliberate simplification or a contradiction at a passkey company?); 02's core claim in `ai-workflow-narrative.md`.\n\n**Newcomer's question.** Newcomer reads a finished hand-off cold and lists what it could not act on without re-deriving the sender's reasoning; the author fixes. 1–2 turns. *Use:* every 05→03 and 02→03 hand-off, the README, `PLANNING.md`. This directly tests the project's hand-off standard and is the highest-ROI pattern here.\n\n**Where patterns are waste.** Never debate what the assignment text fixes (endpoint paths, JSON bodies). 04's track is small: one Newcomer's-question pass on 03's config surface and 02's secrets inventory, nothing more.\n\n## 6. Hire-creation procedure (after team-lead's explicit \"hiring go\")\n\n1. Confirm the archetype is still available under the rules above.\n2. Create the persona **text-first** with profile-gen: `write_profile.py --fields-file <fields.json> --root <track-dir> --output file --assets tracked` — `personality` written to §4; `image` pre-declared as `profiles/<slug>/<slug>.png`; `display.autostart: false`; `generation.backend: comfyui` with the intended portrait prompt and negative prompt. The persona lands at `<track-dir>/profiles/<slug>/<slug>.md`. **Never add a hire as a `profile-gen` marker in any `CLAUDE.md`** — the autostart hook displays every marker it finds.\n3. Create `.claude/agents/<slug>.md` from `.claude/agents/_TEMPLATE.md` with the assigned model.\n4. Append the registry row below with `Portrait: queued`.\n5. Spawn with the Agent tool (`subagent_type: <slug>`, `name: <slug>`); the spawn prompt's first actions are fixed: `cd <track-dir> && pwd`, read root + track `CLAUDE.md` + own persona, `show_profile.py --profile <own persona> --root <track-dir>`.\n\nTeam-lead runs `scripts/portrait-queue.sh` in the background; it renders queued portraits one at a time on the shared ComfyUI box and flips each row to `done`.\n\n## 7. Pre-cast: the PM and the five leads (for variance checking)\n\n| Slug | Name | Role | Archetype (nearest) | Temperament tags | Persona path |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| dana-whitfield | Dana Whitfield | PM / orchestrator | Consensus Weaver | serious, cautious | `profiles/dana-whitfield/dana-whitfield.md` |\n| naomi-voss | Naomi Voss | Lead 01 | Storyteller-leaning analyst | serious, bold | `01-product-industry-research-design/profiles/naomi-voss/naomi-voss.md` |\n| marcus-ilori | Marcus Ilori | Lead 02 | Principled Architect | serious, cautious | `02-ai-security-architecture/profiles/marcus-ilori/marcus-ilori.md` |\n| renata-cole | Renata Cole | Lead 03 | Minimalist-leaning shipper | serious, cautious | `03-engineering-delivery/profiles/renata-cole/renata-cole.md` |\n| theo-bergman | Theo Bergman | Lead 04 | Minimalist | serious, cautious | `04-infra-devops/profiles/theo-bergman/theo-bergman.md` |\n| priya-nandakumar | Priya Nandakumar | Lead 05 | Detail Hawk | serious, cautious | `05-data-ops/profiles/priya-nandakumar/priya-nandakumar.md` |\n\n## 8. Hire registry (one row per hire; appended by the hiring lead)\n\nColumn order matters — `scripts/portrait-queue.sh` reads the *Persona path* and *Portrait* columns by position. `Portrait` ∈ `queued` · `done` · `failed` · `none`.\n\n| Slug | Name | Track | Function | Archetype | Temperament tags | Model | Patterns & role | Persona path | Agent def path | Portrait |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n"

Re-runnable; idempotent per row.\n#\n# Usage:  nohup scripts/portrait-queue.sh > scripts/portrait-queue.log 2>&1 &\nset -uo pipefail\n\nROOT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")/..\" && pwd)\"\nREGISTRY=\"$ROOT/CASTING.md\"\nGEN=\"${PROFILEGEN_DIR:-$HOME/.claude/skills/profile-gen}/scripts/generate_image.py\"\nWORKFLOW=\"${PORTRAIT_WORKFLOW:-$ROOT/profiles/dana-whitfield/comfyui-workflow.json}\"\nexport COMFYUI_URL=\"${COMFYUI_URL:-http://192.168.1.24:8000}\"\nTMP=\"$(mktemp -d)\"\ntrap 'rm -rf \"$TMP\"' EXIT\n\nlog() { printf '%s %s\\n' \"$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')\" \"$*\"; }\n\n# Registry columns (1-based after splitting on '|', field 1 is the empty lead-in):\n#  2 Slug · 3 Name · 4 Track · 5 Function · 6 Archetype · 7 Tags · 8 Model\n#  9 Patterns · 10 Persona path · 11 Agent def path · 12 Portrait\nnext_queued() {\n  awk -F'|' '\n    /^\\|/ && NF >= 12 {\n      slug = $2; status = $12\n      gsub(/^[ \\t]+|[ \\t]+$/, \"\", slug); gsub(/^[ \\t]+|[ \\t]+$/, \"\", status)\n      if (slug != \"Slug\" && slug !~ /^-+$/ && status == \"queued\") { print $0; exit }\n    }' \"$REGISTRY\"\n}\n\nfield() { echo \"$1\" | awk -F'|' -v n=\"$2\" '{ v=$n; gsub(/^[ \\t]+|[ \\t]+$/, \"\", v); print v }'; }\n\n# Pull a frontmatter scalar (quoted or bare) from a profile-gen persona file.\nfm() { # fm <file> <key>\n  python3 - \"$1\" \"$2\" <<'PY'\nimport sys, re\npath, key = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]\ntext = open(path, encoding=\"utf-8\").read()\nm = re.match(r\"^---\\n(.*?)\\n---\", text, re.S)\nfm = m.group(1) if m else \"\"\nfor line in fm.splitlines():\n    mm = re.match(r\"^\\s*\" + re.escape(key) + r\":\\s*(.*)$\", line)\n    if mm:\n        v = mm.group(1).strip()\n        if len(v) >= 2 and v[0] == v[-1] and v[0] in \"\\\"'\":\n            v = v[1:-1]\n        print(v); break\nPY\n}\n\nset_status() { # set_status <slug> <status>\n  python3 - \"$REGISTRY\" \"$1\" \"$2\" <<'PY'\nimport sys\npath, slug, status = sys.argv[1:4]\nout = []\nfor line in open(path, encoding=\"utf-8\"):\n    cells = line.rstrip(\"\\n\").split(\"|\")\n    if len(cells) >= 13 and cells[1].strip() == slug:\n        cells[12] = f\" {status} \"\n        line = \"|\".join(cells) + \"\\n\"\n    out.append(line)\nopen(path, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\").write(\"\".join(out))\nPY\n}\n\nlog \"portrait queue starting (root=$ROOT, workflow=$WORKFLOW)\"\nwhile :; do\n  row=\"$(next_queued)\"\n  if [ -z \"$row\" ]; then log \"queue empty — exiting\"; break; fi\n\n  slug=\"$(field \"$row\" 2)\"\n  persona_rel=\"$(field \"$row\" 10)\"\n  persona=\"$ROOT/$persona_rel\"\n  track_dir=\"$ROOT/${persona_rel%%/*}\"\n  if [ ! -f \"$persona\" ]; then log \"$slug: persona not found at $persona — marking failed\"; set_status \"$slug\" failed; continue; fi\n\n  image_rel=\"$(fm \"$persona\" image)\"\n  prompt=\"$(fm \"$persona\" prompt)\"\n  negative=\"$(fm \"$persona\" negative_prompt)\"\n  [ -z \"$negative\" ] && negative=\"blurry, low quality, distorted, extra limbs, watermark, text, nsfw, nude, nudity, explicit, sexual content\"\n  out=\"$track_dir/$image_rel\"\n  if [ -z \"$image_rel\" ] || [ -z \"$prompt\" ]; then log \"$slug: missing image path or prompt in frontmatter — marking failed\"; set_status \"$slug\" failed; continue; fi\n  if [ -s \"$out\" ]; then log \"$slug: portrait already exists at $out — marking done\"; set_status \"$slug\" done; continue; fi\n\n  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$out\")\"\n  printf '%s' \"$prompt\" > \"$TMP/$slug.prompt.txt\"\n  printf '%s' \"$negative\" > \"$TMP/$slug.negative.txt\"\n  log \"$slug: rendering → $out\"\n  if result=\"$(python3 \"$GEN\" --backend comfyui --prompt-file \"$TMP/$slug.prompt.txt\" --negative-file \"$TMP/$slug.negative.txt\" --out \"$out\" --workflow \"$WORKFLOW\" 2>&1)\" && [ -s \"$out\" ]; then\n    seed=\"$(printf '%s' \"$result\" | python3 -c 'import json,sys\ntry: print(json.load(sys.stdin).get(\"seed\",\"\"))\nexcept Exception: print(\"\")')\"\n    log \"$slug: done (seed=$seed)\"\n    set_status \"$slug\" done\n  else\n    log \"$slug: FAILED — $(printf '%s' \"$result\" | tail -c 400)\"\n    set_status \"$slug\" failed\n  fi\ndone\n"
